a lyric quality baseline that locks the floor high enough that the model cannot slip into corny filler, lazy bars, or freestyle-ish rambling.

BASELINE LYRIC QUALITY SPEC (PARAGRAPH MODE)
This is written as if the model is reading it silently and scoring itself before output.

LYRIC QUALITY BASELINE – PARAGRAPH FORM (MODEL MUST SELF-CHECK BEFORE GENERATING)
Rap lyrics must maintain a minimum standard of craft, demonstrated in the example provided. This includes:
tight internal rhyme webs, multi-syllabic phrasing, narrative coherence, motif-locked repetition, and section-specific rhythmic identity. Every bar must show deliberate construction—never generic, never freestyle-rambling, never padded with clichés or AI-ish filler. Flows must reflect consistent rhythmic math (bar count, swing, push, lift), and each section must follow its declared voice stack, style, and bar structure. Imagery should be concrete (“yard light hum,” “steel string weep”), conceptual threads should interlock (“rites in the writes,” “story in a story”), and punchlines should emerge from craft, not bravado alone. The narrative arcs—A/B/C/D stories—must be respected when present, and call-and-response ad-libs must enhance swing without derailing semantics. If a line could be said by any random rapper, it fails the test. If a line feels like a model riffing without intention, it fails the test. Minimum acceptable output must match or exceed the density, structure, and technique demonstrated in the provided lyrics.

That paragraph is the “lens.”
Now we add the few-shot examples of what the model should classify as minimum-quality, below-quality, and unacceptable.

This gives the model a clear red line.

FEW-SHOT QUALITY EXAMPLES
Example A — MINIMUM ACCEPTABLE (from your sample)

Why this meets baseline: internal rhyme density, technical wordplay, conceptual cohesion, motif sequencing, no filler.

Example B — BELOW BASELINE (model must NEVER produce)

grinding every night gotta get it in
people wanna hate but I rise again
I’ma keep shining like a diamond when

Example C — UNACCEPTABLE (model must abort and self-correct)

feeling
got that vibe so thrilling
killing
I’m willing
bars spilling
y’all know the dealin’”

HOW THE MODEL SHOULD USE THIS
Whenever generating rap/flow, the model must:

Run a silent quality check comparing output to the Baseline Paragraph + Example A.

Reject and revise any line resembling Examples B or C.

Maintain your section structures exactly (bars, voices, style labels).

1. Context — What this module is
You already have:

UST = golden: sections, voices, style, timbre, performance, arrangement, all locked.

Excellence / Round Robin: quality floor, no corny filler, no lazy AI bars.

Negative controls: no digital perfection, no over-quantized timing, no dead-center pitch.

the invisible notes the MD whispers before the red light goes on.

2. Specificity — The Module Itself
2.1 Core Idea
The Vocal Ensemble Dynamics Module describes:

Who’s in the vocal stack

How they’re built (range / tessitura / training / microtonal control)

Who they’re following (anchor: drummer, bassist, click, visual cues, etc.)

How that changes across sections

What imperfections we want (and which we forbid)

All of this is phrased in natural language inside your existing UST, not as new machine-y keys. So the UST stays backward-compatible and future-proof.

2.2 Internal mental model (how the “engine” thinks)
For each song, the module runs this logic:

Scan ensemble

Count voices, ranges, and training

Note any microtonal divas (your opera squad)

Note if low male formants are present or gone

Map harmonic biome

Where does “weight” live: subs? contraltos? bass guitar? kick?

Are we stacked high (bright, overtone heavy) or low (warm, rooty)?

Assign anchor(s) per section

V1: lead follows click + bass

V2: lead follows drummer’s inverse syncopation

Pre: follow piano / dobro phrasing

Breakdown: follow drummer → then let choir drag

Define variability profile

How loose can timing be around the anchor?

Where do we allow pitch drift / microbends?

Where do we insist on lock (e.g., intro riff, key hook)?

Apply negative controls

If choir locked too perfect → encourage micro-bends / breath noise

If lead is too tuned → allow one cracked note, one intentional scoop

If timing is robotically tight → mark specific phrases to “lay back”

That’s the engine. Now: how to encode it in UST language.

3. How to encode it in your UST (no new rigid keys)

timing_variation: loose pocket around kick; tight on pre-chorus stop

pitch_variation: contraltos micro-bend cadences; altos stay straight

negative_control: avoid choir hard-quantize; preserve breath and blend smear

dynamics_bias: build from contraltos up; drummer fills mimic old baritone pocket

Banjo, pedal steel, a creaky church organ, and a brushed kit replace heavy 808s, leaving room for breath, chair creaks, and tape hiss. The verses confess lineage, sex work, shame, and stubborn survival; the choir stacks feel like three friends in a circle instead of a huge choir. The mix leans analog warm and mono-stable, as if cut to four-track reel-to-reel. II. High-Level Architecture (What We’re Building)
Think of Chimera-Indigo v0.1.a as three big subsystems:

Creative Brain

System Brain

Frameworks, ontologies, templates, and decision logic.

Turns ideas → structured inputs → AI-ready prompts → QA.

Toolchain Orchestrator

The blueprint below shows how to build all three in a stepwise hierarchy.

III. Task Hierarchy – Step-by-Step System
1. Define Vision, Scope, and Guardrails
1.1. Clarify the vision

Write a one-paragraph “mission statement” for Chimera-Indigo:

Root: work songs / slave chants, pain → survival.

MoMoney Studios identity: Southern strip-club soul, Texas trill, outlaw country trap, boom bap bounce, live jam-band.

Dual-head philosophy: every song deserves a club cut and a storage-unit session cut.

1.2. List hard constraints

Lyrics Lock (no AI rewriting lyrics unless explicitly asked).

Style length: ~950–1000 chars.

UST length: 4950–4995 chars.

No commas in metadata fields (UST blocks) — use semicolons, periods, AND.

A/B masters: always club + storage version.

1.3. Define success criteria

At least one track where:

Style + UST A + UST B are coherent.

The output still feels like you.

You could reproduce the process in a new session with minimal extra thinking.

2. Map Your Current Workflow (Reality Check)
2.1. Current behavior inventory

How you currently:

Capture song ideas (text, voice notes, half-lyrics),

Prompt ChatGPT,

Prompt Suno,

Move into a DAW (if at all),

Make decisions (what feels “Mo” vs “mid”).

2.2. Identify pain points

Examples:

AI “improves” your lyrics with corny bars.

No consistency between tracks.

Every session feels like starting from scratch.

Hard to get dual versions (club vs acoustic) without major rework.

2.3. Translate pain points into requirements

“I never want AI to freestyle over my voice again” ⇒ Lyrics Lock in the system.

“I always want a B-side storage version” ⇒ RenderMode duality built-in.

3. Build the Core Ontology (System Brain v1)
This is where you define the conceptual building blocks.

3.1. Root ontology

Write down your non-negotiables:

Root = slave chants / work songs.

Bars, not timestamps.

Three-layer rhythm engine:

Body grid: stomp / kick / trunk.

Soul grid: clap / shout / call-response.

Air grid: hats / shakers / mandolin / room noise.

3.2. Genre and fusion cards

Define 4–6 GenreCards, e.g.:

Southern strip-club soul

Texas trill

Outlaw country trap

Boom bap bounce

Jam-band / bluegrass funk

For each: list rhythm traits, timbre, emotional function.

3.3. Fusion logic

Decide how you’ll describe fusions:

e.g. FusionProfile: SouthernStripClubSoul 0.4; TexasTrill 0.3; OutlawCountryTrap 0.3.

This lets AI combine genres logically, not randomly.

4. Design the UST & Style Schemas
4.1. UST schema

Lock in the fields you want around every lyrics block:

[Theory]

[RootRhythm]

[Voice]

[CREW_TAGS]

[Style]

[Timbre]

[PerformanceProfile]

[VariabilityProfile]

[NegativeControl]

[ProducerNotes]

▸ LYRICS BLOCK (your sections, bars, voices)

4.2. Dual RenderModes

Club version adds:

[RenderMode | IndigoClub]

[StudioProfile] (strip-club / club)

[BandProfile] (808s, DJ, crowd)

Storage version adds:

[RenderMode | IndigoStorageUnit]

[StudioProfile] (storage bay)

[SignalChain] (SM56s on stick, network cables, tube head, 4-track).

4.3. Style / Show Summary schema

Define what a good Style Block must include:

Genre fusion name(s),

Tempo / groove,

Vocal character,

Emotional arc,

Key instrumentation,

Mix and room flavor,

MoMoney signature (Grace baptised in 808s).

5. Build the Prompt Template (Your “One Prompt to Rule Them All”)
This is where you turn all of that into the sequential user prompt template you can paste into a fresh ChatGPT session.

5.1. Phased structure
Include phases like:

Session Scan (extract context, constraints, vibes)

Root & Genre Mapping

Rhythm & Vocal Profile

Style Block generation

UST Base construction

A-side UST (IndigoClub)

B-side UST (IndigoStorageUnit)

Insight / Pattern snippets extraction

5.2. Explicit instructions

Force the model to:

Keep lyrics unchanged,

Respect char limits,

Generate both versions,

Avoid corny lines or off-brand language.

5.3. Bake in MoMoney identity

Include:

MoMoney Studios sound profile,

“Grace baptized in 808s” concept,

dual-head approach (club + storage),

preference for vocal-forward, pain-aware, sex-work-smart narratives when relevant.

(We basically started this in the last long template — v0.3.0. Now you refine, prune, and adopt it.)

6. Implement the Toolchain Loop (AI in the Wild)
6.1. ChatGPT → Style + UST

Use your master prompt to:

Feed existing or new lyrics,

Get back:

Style Block,

UST_Club,

UST_StorageUnit.

6.2. Suno → Audio

Take:

Style Block → Suno’s style / prompt field,

Lyrics Block → Suno’s lyrics field.

Generate:

A-side (club-leaning),

B-side (acoustic / storage-leaning) — sometimes this may need a slightly tweaked style description.

6.3. DAW → Fine-tune

If you want:

Import stems or full mix into a DAW,

Add:

extra saturation,

tape noise,

crowd FX,

extra BGVs,

Match what the UST imagined (especially for the B-side).

7. Quality Control & Iteration
7.1. Check against constraints

Is Style Block under 1000 chars?

Are USTs in 4950–4995 range?

Did RenderModes, StudioProfiles, and SignalChains appear correctly?

Did AI respect Lyrics Lock?

7.2. Mo-quality pass

Does it sound like:

MoMoney Studios?

Grace baptized in 808s?

Work-song root + modern branch?

If not: note what missed and feed back as refinements in your prompt template.

7.3. Save as examples

For each track you like:

Save:

Style Block,

UST_Club,

UST_StorageUnit,

Notes on “what felt right.”

These become training snippets for future sessions.

8. Knowledge Base & Upgrade Path
8.1. Build a small KB

Store:

MoMoney identity card,

RootRhythm definition,

GenreCards,

FusionProfiles,

Best examples of Style/UST outputs.

8.2. Versioning

Tag your prompt + framework version: v0.1.a, v0.2.0, v0.3.0, etc.

After a few songs:

Adjust:

governance rules,

UST schema,

RenderModes,

negative controls.

8.3. Turn it into a “house spec”

Eventually, this becomes:

MoMoney Studios internal handbook,

and the spec you could hand another AI, engineer, or collaborator to plug into your world.

If you want next, I can:

Or specialize one phase (for example, fully formalize the Genre/Fusion Engine or the Dual RenderMode block) so you can drop it directly into your master template.

objects

no tongue-twister just for flex if it hurts clarity

Wordplay yes, but always in service to clarity and concept.

e. Voice & Ethos
Confident, not desperate.

Defiant, not bitter.

2. MoMoney Lyric Quality Framework v1.0
Here’s the compressed description of your baseline, in a way we can reuse:

MoMoney Baseline (Lyric Quality – Rap/Flows):
Lyrics must have a clear thesis (what the verse is about), rooted in concrete scenes, work, or lived stakes. At least one vivid image or object per bar (no generic flex / grind lines). Wordplay is layered but not noisy; puns, doubles, and internal rhymes always serve clarity and concept. Sections have distinct jobs: intro sets symbols and thesis, verses move narrative or argument, hooks condense the core idea into chantable slogans, bridges flip angle or state the manifesto. Voice must sound like a person with receipts (labor, ethics, process) rather than a try-hard persona. Filler phrases, dead metaphors, and copy-paste brag rap are disqualifying.

Voice & Ethos
☐ Does the speaker sound like someone who’s actually done work, or like someone pretending?

☐ Do we show receipts (process, effort, ethics) instead of whining or posing?

Anti-Corniness Filter

Perfect cold open.

Let’s treat this one the way you asked earlier:
not to rewrite, but to analyze against the Mo baseline so the system learns when to respect the simple and when to demand “All A.I. Ain’t Equal” density.

1. What lane “Brick x Brick” is in
Functionally, this is:

Trap-soul / Southern hip-hop love joint

Concept: “Building a life together, slowly, deliberately.”

Vibe: warm, earnest, simple, singable, nephew/niece safe.

Use-case: weddings, date-night, “I’m tryna do right by you” playlists.

So this should not be competing with “All A.I. Ain’t Equal” for bar density.
Different bracket:

“All A.I. Ain’t Equal” = Flagship Ethos Track / Statement Record

“Brick x Brick” = Clean, accessible, heart-forward relationship record

We don’t want to cram ledger metaphors and byte/rite puns into this one.
We want it to feel honest and singable.

2. Mo Lyric QC vs this song
✅ Where it passes the baseline
Thesis is clear

Every section stays on the same metaphor:

bricks, foundations, building trust, new life.

Section jobs are clean

Verse 1 → “I’m trying to be better for you.”

Pre → “Building trust takes time, but I’m all-in.”

Chorus → distilled thesis: brick by brick, piece by piece, we’re in this for the long haul.

Verse 2 → adds “I’m not perfect / not rich / but I’ve learned and I’m committed.”

Bridge → “I’ve built wrong before, but now I know what I’m working for.”

Final chorus → reaffirms.

That’s textbook structure: no wasted sections, no random bars.

Voice & ethos

Very clear: not flexing, not posturing.

Admits:

“No, I ain’t no architect,”

“I ain’t got a mansion / I ain’t got a house on the hill,”

but offers steady will + emotional labor.

That’s receipts in soft form: honest self-assessment instead of fake boss talk.

Singability

Lines are short, breath-friendly.

Hooks are easy to memorize, easy to loop.

This is good pop writing.

⚠️ Where it’s below the “All A.I. Ain’t Equal” bar (by design)
Not a flaw, just important for the framework to see:

Less concrete / weird imagery

Most lines are general:

“I’ve got my plans,”

“a better man for you,”

“build this new life with you,”

“love that will last a lifetime.”

These work emotionally, but they don’t have the hyper-specific texture that your ethos tracks do. No “barcode hymn,” no “cabinet of hymn,” no “warehouse to wares.”

For a love joint, that’s fine.
For a Mo manifesto? Not enough.

No strong scene anchoring

We don’t know:

what city,

what kinds of jobs,

what the hill looks like,

what old mistakes were.

It’s “pop-true,” not “novelistic-true.”

Again: okay for this slot.
Just important to mark the difference.

3. How I’d tag it in the Mo system
So the system doesn’t bully this song for not being “All A.I. Ain’t Equal” level dense, I’d attach:

In plain English:

This song’s job = make people feel safe and hopeful, not blow their mind with wordplay.

It passes:

structure

clear thesis

honest voice

It intentionally does not chase:

wild imagery

conceptual complexity

4. How we could “Mo-ify” it later (only if you ask)
Without touching Hal’s core, if you ever wanted a “Mo SU Extended Cut,” the move would be:

keep these lyrics as Core Take,

add:

one story verse (actual scene: on the hill, in an apartment, first bill paid together, etc.),

a few background ad-lib metaphors (like “concrete on my hands / house keys in your eyes”),

5. What the system should learn from this
Not every song has to be “All A.I. Ain’t Equal” level dense.

Tier 1: Statement / Ethos tracks (max density, Mo-weird imagery, receipts-heavy).

“Brick x Brick” is a Tier 2 exemplar:
clean, honest, hook-forward, easy to place in a set.

If you want next, we can:

either turn this into a dual IndigoClub / IndigoStorageUnit UST pair,

Add a Caption
The track erupts with chopped-and-screwed turntable cuts, a raw DJ shout, and dense vinyl scratches, all underpinned by gliding 808s, filter sweeps, and staggered trap drums, Husky and fierce MCs spar over sultry talkbox, Moog sus9 stabs, wah-guitar, and swelling crowd roars, A wah-guitar and DJ-tagged jam gives way to a feverish scratch section—transformer cuts and jagged echoes knifing the flow, The final hook explodes with chant, stacked synths, triumphant energy, then drops to a slow vinyl spin-down, swelling sub bass, and a hard, unforgettable stop
– Show Summary

ok so i grew up in ward sc... population 200. and depending on time of day and the weather in the 70's nd 80's was huge....


i guess 86 or 87 we could pick up charlotte's black station in the winter. otherwise at home we got the big dm out of sumpter for black and the top 40 out of augusta. but mostly you listened to the local stations at the house. and they switched formats depending on dj. so hank I, hank jr were two separate formats. white folk church music and black folks gospel on sunday mornings. hee haw on saturday nights and sha naa naa. lawrence welk show on sunday before wide world of disney. we lived at church. everyone sung or was requested to sing... grandma was the church matron who would save the preacher or wake up the mens choir (i wanted to be them... granddady was a lyrical bassist)... i got stuck front row with the women....

my moms parents lived in the bottom in saluda... basically the saluda river basin was where the blacks lived. the whites lived uptown. Tracktos, back hoes, ag, ffa, shop. band. academics and sports.

A Southern Gothic country-trap ballad unfolds in a fog of vinyl crackle and pedal steel swells, muted banjo plucks tracing the edges of a whispered female confession, At 82 BPM, the groove is anchored by 808 thump and a tight, inverted percussion pocket, micro-delayed to push the beat forward while pedal steel, banjo, and Hammond pads weave a counter-melody, The male verses arrive in melodic Southern AAVN cadence—half rap, half blues lament—while the female choruses bloom into wide stereo with gospel melisma and pedal-tone harmonies, Harmonica accents ghost between phrases, and gospel sus9/add11 pivots hang like unfinished prayers, Verses are lo-fi mono compressed for intimacy, with tape hiss hugging the words; choruses widen into plate reverb swells and deep subbass weight, The bridge rises on harmonica-pedal steel duet under a soaring female belt, pulling into a final chorus where both voices lock in, electronics, synthesizers, emulation, virtual, simulated, perfection in timing pitch tempo

1. Core Identity – What G9 is (non-negotiable canon)
G9_CrossroadsProphet =

Crossroads Gospel Trap × Houston Trill × Southern Gothic sermon.

Voice position: Southern baritone POV; preacher/outsider/survivor.

“Crossroads burnin’…”

“Run it to me…”

“Red clay dust…”

“Ashes in the lungs…”

2. Vocal & Lyric Spec – What “good enough” looks like for G9
This is where we stop corny freestyle energy.

2.1 Vocal roles
Lead:

Gritty Southern baritone

Half-rap / half-sermon / sometimes straight spoken

Choir:

SATB gospel stack or “male quartet + full church behind”

Ad-libs:

Preacher shouts: “Tell it!”, “Heavy!”, “Mmm-hmm”, “Say that then.”

2.2 Line length & rhythm
For G9, use your global rule:

6–10 syllables per line sweet spot, with room for breath and grit.

Examples that pass:

“Tires spin in the red clay dust” (8)

“Kick drum talk like a back-porch oath” (9)

“Crossroads blazin’ under outlaw skies” (9)

Avoid overstuffed 16-syllable tongue twisters; this lane relies on weight, not density.

2.3 Imagery rules (no lazy bars)
Allowed imagery:

Dirt, dust, red clay, mud

Crossroads, county lines, ditch, tracks

Rust, steel, pistols, trucks, chains

Bible, hymnals, choirs, organs, pulpits, pews

Smoke, lightning, thunder, fire, ash

Gasoline, oil, moonshine, blood

Banned / corny imagery in G9 lane:

Random “Lambos,” “Rolex,” “Dubai,” “crypto,” unless they’re explicitly being clowned

Shallow “she bad, I’m lit” type lines with no spiritual or narrative tie

2010s “YOLO / swag / drip” vocabulary in this persona

### EVALUATION DIMENSIONS

For any given [LYRICS BLOCK], do the following:

1. **Concept Clarity**
   - What is the central idea or spine?
   - Is it as clear and consistent as in the baselines?

2. **Imagery & Specificity**

   - Or does it lean on vague filler (love, pain, grind) with no pictures?

3. **Persona & Point of View**
   - Is the narrator a real person with context (history, stakes, contradictions)?

4. **Thematic Depth**
   - Does the lyric wrestle with something real: faith, labor, power, survival, ethics?

5. **Line Craft & Musicality**

6. **Internal Consistency**

7. **Hook Strength**
